+'use client'
+
+// src/components/command-centre/wiki-graph/WikiGraphCanvas.tsx
+//
+// Obsidian-style interactive force-directed graph of the wiki knowledge base
+// (UNI-2304). Canvas renderer (chosen over SVG for the ~460-node / thousands-
+// of-edge real wiki). d3-force drives the layout and stays warm so the graph
+// gently drifts; d3-zoom owns pan/zoom; node drag + hover + click are handled
+// on the same canvas with a single owner per event type (zoom's filter yields
+// pointer-downs that land on a node to the manual drag handler).
+
+import { useEffect, useRef, useState, useCallback } from 'react'
+import { useRouter } from 'next/navigation'
+import {
+  forceSimulation,
+  forceManyBody,
+  forceLink,
+  forceCenter,
+  forceCollide,
+  type Simulation,
+  type SimulationNodeDatum,
+  type SimulationLinkDatum,
+} from 'd3-force'
+import { select } from 'd3-selection'
+import { zoom as d3zoom, zoomIdentity, type ZoomTransform, type D3ZoomEvent } from 'd3-zoom'
+
+export interface WikiGraphNode {
+  id: string
+  title: string
+  slug: string
+  tags: string[]
+  degree: number
+}
+export interface WikiGraphEdge {
+  source: string
+  target: string
+}
+
+type SimNode = WikiGraphNode & SimulationNodeDatum
+type SimLink = SimulationLinkDatum<SimNode>
+
+// Candy-deck palette (matches command-deck.module.css tokens).
+const COL = {
+  node: '#2dbb57',
+  nodeDim: 'rgba(45,187,87,0.18)',
+  link: 'rgba(90,107,98,0.28)',
+  linkDim: 'rgba(90,107,98,0.06)',
+  linkHi: '#f4820f',
+  focus: '#15803d',
+  label: '#14241b',
+  labelDim: 'rgba(20,36,27,0.28)',
+}
+
+/** Keep the simulation perpetually warm for gentle, living motion. */
+const KEEP_ALIVE = 0.012
+/** Screen-pixels of slop added to a node's hit radius. */
+const HIT_PADDING = 6
+
+function nodeRadius(degree: number): number {
+  return 3 + Math.sqrt(degree) * 1.6
+}
+
+interface Props {
+  nodes: WikiGraphNode[]
+  edges: WikiGraphEdge[]
+}
+
+interface Tooltip {
+  x: number
+  y: number
+  title: string
+  tags: string[]
+  degree: number
+}
+
+export function WikiGraphCanvas({ nodes, edges }: Props) {
+  const router = useRouter()
+  const wrapRef = useRef<HTMLDivElement>(null)
+  const canvasRef = useRef<HTMLCanvasElement>(null)
+  const [tooltip, setTooltip] = useState<Tooltip | null>(null)
+
+  // Mutable refs shared across the render loop and event handlers.
+  const simNodesRef = useRef<SimNode[]>([])
+  const simLinksRef = useRef<SimLink[]>([])
+  const adjacencyRef = useRef<Map<string, Set<string>>>(new Map())
+  const transformRef = useRef<ZoomTransform>(zoomIdentity)
+  const hoverRef = useRef<SimNode | null>(null)
+  const draggingRef = useRef<SimNode | null>(null)
+  const sizeRef = useRef({ w: 0, h: 0 })
+  const simRef = useRef<Simulation<SimNode, SimLink> | null>(null)
+
+  /** World-space node under a screen pointer (canvas-relative px), or null. */
+  const nodeAtScreen = useCallback((px: number, py: number): SimNode | null => {
+    const t = transformRef.current
+    const wx = t.invertX(px)
+    const wy = t.invertY(py)
+    let best: SimNode | null = null
+    let bestD = Infinity
+    for (const n of simNodesRef.current) {
+      if (n.x === undefined || n.y === undefined) continue
+      const r = nodeRadius(n.degree) + HIT_PADDING / t.k
+      const dx = n.x - wx
+      const dy = n.y - wy
+      const d = dx * dx + dy * dy
+      if (d < r * r && d < bestD) {
+        bestD = d
+        best = n
+      }
+    }
+    return best
+  }, [])
+
+  useEffect(() => {
+    const canvas = canvasRef.current
+    const wrap = wrapRef.current
+    if (!canvas || !wrap) return
+
+    const ctx = canvas.getContext('2d')
+    if (!ctx) return
+
+    // Build simulation datasets (clone so d3 can mutate freely).
+    const simNodes: SimNode[] = nodes.map((n) => ({ ...n }))
+    const byId = new Map(simNodes.map((n) => [n.id, n]))
+    const simLinks: SimLink[] = edges
+      .filter((e) => byId.has(e.source) && byId.has(e.target))
+      .map((e) => ({ source: e.source, target: e.target }))
+    simNodesRef.current = simNodes
+    simLinksRef.current = simLinks
+
+    // Adjacency for hover-neighbour highlighting.
+    const adjacency = new Map<string, Set<string>>()
+    for (const n of simNodes) adjacency.set(n.id, new Set())
+    for (const e of edges) {
+      adjacency.get(e.source)?.add(e.target)
+      adjacency.get(e.target)?.add(e.source)
+    }
+    adjacencyRef.current = adjacency
+
+    // Size + HiDPI backing store.
+    function resize() {
+      const rect = wrap!.getBoundingClientRect()
+      const dpr = Math.min(window.devicePixelRatio || 1, 2)
+      sizeRef.current = { w: rect.width, h: rect.height }
+      canvas!.width = Math.floor(rect.width * dpr)
+      canvas!.height = Math.floor(rect.height * dpr)
+      canvas!.style.width = `${rect.width}px`
+      canvas!.style.height = `${rect.height}px`
+      ctx!.setTransform(dpr, 0, 0, dpr, 0, 0)
+      // World-space centroid stays at the origin â€” the initial zoom transform
+      // below already translates that origin to the screen centre. Centring
+      // the force here too would double-translate and start the cluster
+      // off-centre.
+      sim.force('center', forceCenter(0, 0))
+    }
+
+    const sim = forceSimulation(simNodes)
+      .force('charge', forceManyBody().strength(-42).distanceMax(320))
+      .force('link', forceLink<SimNode, SimLink>(simLinks).id((d) => d.id).distance(46).strength(0.35))
+      .force('collide', forceCollide<SimNode>().radius((d) => nodeRadius(d.degree) + 2))
+      .force('center', forceCenter(0, 0))
+      .alphaTarget(KEEP_ALIVE)
+    simRef.current = sim
+    resize()
+
+    function draw() {
+      const { w, h } = sizeRef.current
+      const t = transformRef.current
+      ctx!.save()
+      ctx!.clearRect(0, 0, w, h)
+      ctx!.translate(t.x, t.y)
+      ctx!.scale(t.k, t.k)
+
+      const hover = hoverRef.current
+      const neighbours = hover ? adjacencyRef.current.get(hover.id) : null
+
+      // Links.
+      ctx!.lineWidth = 1 / t.k
+      for (const l of simLinksRef.current) {
+        const s = l.source as SimNode
+        const tg = l.target as SimNode
+        if (s.x === undefined || tg.x === undefined) continue
+        const touchesHover = hover && (s.id === hover.id || tg.id === hover.id)
+        ctx!.strokeStyle = hover ? (touchesHover ? COL.linkHi : COL.linkDim) : COL.link
+        ctx!.beginPath()
+        ctx!.moveTo(s.x, s.y!)
+        ctx!.lineTo(tg.x, tg.y!)
+        ctx!.stroke()
+      }
+
+      // Nodes.
+      for (const n of simNodesRef.current) {
+        if (n.x === undefined || n.y === undefined) continue
+        const isHover = hover?.id === n.id
+        const isNeighbour = neighbours?.has(n.id) ?? false
+        const active = !hover || isHover || isNeighbour
+        ctx!.beginPath()
+        ctx!.arc(n.x, n.y, nodeRadius(n.degree), 0, Math.PI * 2)
+        ctx!.fillStyle = active ? (isHover ? COL.focus : COL.node) : COL.nodeDim
+        ctx!.fill()
+        if (isHover) {
+          ctx!.lineWidth = 2 / t.k
+          ctx!.strokeStyle = COL.focus
+          ctx!.stroke()
+        }
+      }
+
+      // Labels â€” only when zoomed in enough, or for the hovered subgraph.
+      const showAllLabels = t.k > 1.6
+      if (showAllLabels || hover) {
+        ctx!.font = `${11 / t.k}px var(--font-chakra), system-ui, sans-serif`
+        ctx!.textAlign = 'center'
+        ctx!.textBaseline = 'top'
+        for (const n of simNodesRef.current) {
+          if (n.x === undefined || n.y === undefined) continue
+          const isHover = hover?.id === n.id
+          const isNeighbour = neighbours?.has(n.id) ?? false
+          if (hover && !isHover && !isNeighbour) continue
+          if (!hover && !showAllLabels) continue
+          ctx!.fillStyle = hover && !isHover && !isNeighbour ? COL.labelDim : COL.label
+          ctx!.fillText(n.title, n.x, n.y + nodeRadius(n.degree) + 1)
+        }
+      }
+
+      ctx!.restore()
+    }
+
+    sim.on('tick', draw)
+
+    // â”€â”€ Pan / zoom (d3-zoom). Filter yields node-hits to the drag handler. â”€â”€
+    const zoomBehavior = d3zoom<HTMLCanvasElement, unknown>()
+      .scaleExtent([0.15, 6])
+      .filter((event: Event) => {
+        // Block the browser context menu / right-click; allow wheel + dblclick.
+        if (event.type === 'wheel' || event.type === 'dblclick') return true
+        const me = event as MouseEvent
+        if (me.button != null && me.button !== 0) return false
+        // Pointer/mouse down: only pan when NOT starting on a node.
+        const rect = canvas!.getBoundingClientRect()
+        return nodeAtScreen(me.clientX - rect.left, me.clientY - rect.top) === null
+      })
+      .on('zoom', (event: D3ZoomEvent<HTMLCanvasElement, unknown>) => {
+        transformRef.current = event.transform
+        draw()
+      })
+
+    const selection = select(canvas)
+    selection.call(zoomBehavior)
+    // Start centred with a gentle initial zoom.
+    const rect0 = wrap.getBoundingClientRect()
+    selection.call(zoomBehavior.transform, zoomIdentity.translate(rect0.width / 2, rect0.height / 2).scale(0.85))
+
+    // â”€â”€ Node drag (manual, pointer-captured). â”€â”€
+    function pointerToScreen(e: PointerEvent): { x: number; y: number } {
+      const rect = canvas!.getBoundingClientRect()
+      return { x: e.clientX - rect.left, y: e.clientY - rect.top }
+    }
+
+    function onPointerDown(e: PointerEvent) {
+      if (e.button !== 0) return
+      const { x, y } = pointerToScreen(e)
+      const hit = nodeAtScreen(x, y)
+      if (!hit) return // empty space â†’ zoom handles panning
+      e.preventDefault()
+      canvas!.setPointerCapture(e.pointerId)
+      draggingRef.current = hit
+      sim.alphaTarget(0.3).restart()
+      const t = transformRef.current
+      hit.fx = t.invertX(x)
+      hit.fy = t.invertY(y)
+    }
+
+    function onPointerMove(e: PointerEvent) {
+      const { x, y } = pointerToScreen(e)
+      const drag = draggingRef.current
+      if (drag) {
+        const t = transformRef.current
+        drag.fx = t.invertX(x)
+        drag.fy = t.invertY(y)
+        return
+      }
+      // Hover.
+      const hit = nodeAtScreen(x, y)
+      if (hit !== hoverRef.current) {
+        hoverRef.current = hit
+        canvas!.style.cursor = hit ? 'pointer' : 'grab'
+        draw()
+      }
+      if (hit) {
+        setTooltip({ x, y, title: hit.title, tags: hit.tags, degree: hit.degree })
+      } else {
+        setTooltip(null)
+      }
+    }
+
+    function onPointerUp(e: PointerEvent) {
+      const drag = draggingRef.current
+      if (!drag) return
+      draggingRef.current = null
+      try {
+        canvas!.releasePointerCapture(e.pointerId)
+      } catch {
+        /* pointer already released */
+      }
+      sim.alphaTarget(KEEP_ALIVE)
+      // Release the pin so the node rejoins the living simulation.
+      drag.fx = null
+      drag.fy = null
+    }
+
+    // Click-to-navigate: a clean tap on a node (no meaningful drag).
+    let downPt: { x: number; y: number } | null = null
+    function onClickDown(e: PointerEvent) {
+      downPt = pointerToScreen(e)
+    }
+    function onClickUp(e: PointerEvent) {
+      if (!downPt) return
+      const up = pointerToScreen(e)
+      const moved = Math.hypot(up.x - downPt.x, up.y - downPt.y)
+      downPt = null
+      if (moved > 4) return
+      const hit = nodeAtScreen(up.x, up.y)
+      if (hit) router.push(`/founder/wiki/${encodeURIComponent(hit.slug)}`)
+    }
+
+    canvas.addEventListener('pointerdown', onPointerDown)
+    canvas.addEventListener('pointerdown', onClickDown)
+    canvas.addEventListener('pointermove', onPointerMove)
+    canvas.addEventListener('pointerup', onPointerUp)
+    canvas.addEventListener('pointerup', onClickUp)
+    // KI-003 (inherited, NOT introduced): this listener is anonymous and the
+    // cleanup below never removes it, so it accumulates each time the effect
+    // reruns on [nodes, edges, ...]. Cross-vendor review flagged it as a hard
+    // violation and it is one â€” in the BASELINE. This file is a byte-identical
+    // port and the governing instruction is "port faithfully, including existing
+    // behaviour"; fixing it here would fork the port from its source. Founder
+    // ruled 2026-08-01: declare, do not fix. The fix belongs upstream in
+    // Authority-Site, and this port inherits it when the baseline moves.
+    canvas.addEventListener('pointerleave', () => {
+      hoverRef.current = null
+      setTooltip(null)
+      draw()
+    })
+    canvas.style.cursor = 'grab'
+
+    const ro = new ResizeObserver(() => {
+      resize()
+      draw()
+    })
+    ro.observe(wrap)
+
+    return () => {
+      sim.stop()
+      ro.disconnect()
+      selection.on('.zoom', null)
+      canvas.removeEventListener('pointerdown', onPointerDown)
+      canvas.removeEventListener('pointerdown', onClickDown)
+      canvas.removeEventListener('pointermove', onPointerMove)
+      canvas.removeEventListener('pointerup', onPointerUp)
+      canvas.removeEventListener('pointerup', onClickUp)
+    }
+  }, [nodes, edges, nodeAtScreen, router])
+
+  return (
+    <div
+      ref={wrapRef}
+      style={{
+        position: 'relative',
+        width: '100%',
+        height: '100%',
+        minHeight: 0,
+        overflow: 'hidden',
+        borderRadius: 2,
+        border: '1px solid rgba(45,187,87,0.20)',
+        background: '#fffdf7',
+      }}
+    >
+      <canvas ref={canvasRef} style={{ display: 'block', touchAction: 'none' }} />
+      {tooltip && (
+        <div
+          style={{
+            position: 'absolute',
+            // KI-004 (inherited): reading sizeRef during render is a real
+            // react-hooks/refs violation, present verbatim at this same line in the
+            // baseline. Suppressed rather than fixed for the same reason as KI-003
+            // above: this is a faithful port and the fix belongs upstream. The
+            // suppression is scoped to the single line below, so any NEW
+            // ref-in-render in this file still fails lint.
+            // eslint-disable-next-line react-hooks/refs
+            left: Math.min(tooltip.x + 14, (sizeRef.current.w || 0) - 220),
+            top: tooltip.y + 14,
+            pointerEvents: 'none',
+            maxWidth: 220,
+            padding: '8px 10px',
+            borderRadius: 2,
+            border: '1px solid rgba(45,187,87,0.25)',
+            background: '#ffffff',
+            boxShadow: '0 6px 18px rgba(20,36,27,0.12)',
+            fontSize: 12,
+            color: '#14241b',
+            zIndex: 5,
+          }}
+        >
+          <div style={{ fontWeight: 600, marginBottom: 2 }}>{tooltip.title}</div>
+          <div style={{ color: '#5a6b62', fontSize: 11 }}>
+            {tooltip.degree} {tooltip.degree === 1 ? 'link' : 'links'}
+          </div>
+          {tooltip.tags.length > 0 && (
+            <div style={{ display: 'flex', flexWrap: 'wrap', gap: 3, marginTop: 4 }}>
+              {tooltip.tags.slice(0, 6).map((tag) => (
+                <span
+                  key={tag}
+                  style={{
+                    fontSize: 10,
+                    padding: '1px 5px',
+                    borderRadius: 2,
+                    background: 'rgba(45,187,87,0.10)',
+                    color: '#15803d',
+                    border: '1px solid rgba(45,187,87,0.18)',
+                  }}
+                >
+                  {tag}
+                </span>
+              ))}
+            </div>
+          )}
+        </div>
+      )}
+    </div>
+  )
+}

+'use client'
+
+// src/components/command-centre/wiki-graph/WikiGraphTile.tsx
+//
+// Command-deck tile for the Wiki Graph View (UNI-2304). Client-fetches the
+// founder-scoped graph summary and links to the full interactive page.
+// Error-contained: a failed fetch degrades to an honest message, never a fake
+// count.
+
+import { useEffect, useState } from 'react'
+import Link from 'next/link'
+import { SourceBadge, type SourceMode } from '@/components/command-centre/SourceBadge'
+
+interface GraphSummary {
+  pageCount: number
+  edgeCount: number
+  lastSync: string | null
+}
+
+export function WikiGraphTile() {
+  const [summary, setSummary] = useState<GraphSummary | null>(null)
+  const [loading, setLoading] = useState(true)
+  const [error, setError] = useState<string | null>(null)
+
+  useEffect(() => {
+    let alive = true
+    async function load() {
+      try {
+        const res = await fetch('/api/command-centre/wiki-graph')
+        if (!res.ok) throw new Error(`HTTP ${res.status}`)
+        const data = (await res.json()) as GraphSummary
+        if (alive) {
+          setSummary({ pageCount: data.pageCount, edgeCount: data.edgeCount, lastSync: data.lastSync })
+          setError(null)
+        }
+      } catch (e) {
+        if (alive) setError(e instanceof Error ? e.message : 'load failed')
+      } finally {
+        if (alive) setLoading(false)
+      }
+    }
+    load()
+    return () => {
+      alive = false
+    }
+  }, [])
+
+  const mode: SourceMode = loading ? 'loading' : error || !summary ? 'degraded' : 'live'
+
+  return (
+    <section style={{ display: 'flex', flexDirection: 'column', gap: 8 }}>
+      <div style={{ display: 'flex', justifyContent: 'space-between', alignItems: 'center' }}>
+        <h3 style={{ color: 'var(--deck-text)', fontSize: 14, fontWeight: 700, margin: 0 }}>Wiki Graph</h3>
+        <SourceBadge mode={mode} label="Wiki" lastUpdatedAt={summary?.lastSync ?? undefined} />
+      </div>
+
+      {error ? (
+        <p style={{ color: 'var(--deck-abort-text)', fontSize: 12, margin: 0 }}>Could not load wiki graph: {error}</p>
+      ) : (
+        <p style={{ color: 'var(--deck-muted)', fontSize: 12, margin: 0 }}>
+          <b style={{ color: 'var(--deck-text)' }}>{summary?.pageCount ?? 0}</b> pages Â·{' '}
+          <b style={{ color: 'var(--deck-text)' }}>{summary?.edgeCount ?? 0}</b> links in the knowledge base
+        </p>
+      )}
+
+      <Link
+        href="/command-centre/wiki-graph"
+        style={{
+          alignSelf: 'flex-start',
+          fontSize: 12,
+          padding: '5px 12px',
+          borderRadius: 2,
+          border: '1px solid var(--deck-line)',
+          color: 'var(--deck-cyan-text)',
+          textDecoration: 'none',
+        }}
+      >
+        Open interactive graph â†’
+      </Link>
+    </section>
+  )
+}

+// src/lib/command-centre/tools/catalogue.ts
+//
+// READ-ONLY tool-catalogue discovery for the Nexus Command Centre.
+//
+// This module LISTS the tool sources the command centre is aware of. It NEVER
+// invokes, executes, spawns, or connects to any tool â€” discovery only. It proves
+// "access to all tools" with zero execution risk.
+//
+// Sources:
+//   (a) A static, known set of MCP servers + Hermes toolsets + code-execution
+//       surfaces from the Nexus spec.
+//   (b) Optional read-only enrichment: if a Hermes config file is present we read
+//       *server names only* (never values/secrets) and append any not already known.
+//
+// risk_class / source enums mirror cc_tools in the master spec.
+
+import { readFile } from 'node:fs/promises'
+import path from 'node:path'
+import os from 'node:os'
+
+export type ToolSource = 'hermes' | 'mcp' | 'project' | 'codex' | 'claude-code' | 'local'
+
+export type ToolRiskClass =
+  | 'read'
+  | 'write-local'
+  | 'write-shared'
+  | 'external'
+  | 'destructive'
+
+export interface CommandCentreTool {
+  /** Stable key for the tool / toolset / surface. */
+  tool_key: string
+  /** Where the capability comes from. */
+  source: ToolSource
+  /** Human-readable description. */
+  description: string
+  /** Risk classification (conservative default). */
+  risk_class: ToolRiskClass
+  /** Whether a human approval is required before any (future) invocation. */
+  approval_required: boolean
+  /**
+   * Discovery is list-only. This flag is ALWAYS false in the starter slice â€”
+   * the catalogue never marks anything as invocable.
+   */
+  invocable: false
+}
+
+/**
+ * The static, authoritative known set. MCP servers, Hermes toolsets, and
+ * code-execution surfaces named in the Nexus spec (Section "access to all tools").
+ */
+export const KNOWN_TOOLS: readonly CommandCentreTool[] = [
+  // --- MCP servers --------------------------------------------------------
+  { tool_key: 'linear', source: 'mcp', description: 'Linear MCP â€” issues, projects, cycles (read/write).', risk_class: 'write-shared', approval_required: true, invocable: false },
+  { tool_key: 'supabase', source: 'mcp', description: 'Supabase MCP â€” database, migrations, advisors.', risk_class: 'write-shared', approval_required: true, invocable: false },
+  { tool_key: 'github', source: 'mcp', description: 'GitHub MCP â€” repos, PRs, issues, code search.', risk_class: 'write-shared', approval_required: true, invocable: false },
+  { tool_key: 'google', source: 'mcp', description: 'Google MCP â€” Gmail / Drive / Calendar surfaces.', risk_class: 'external', approval_required: true, invocable: false },
+  { tool_key: 'slack', source: 'mcp', description: 'Slack MCP â€” channels, messages, canvases.', risk_class: 'external', approval_required: true, invocable: false },
+  { tool_key: 'chrome', source: 'mcp', description: 'Chrome MCP â€” browser automation / page reading.', risk_class: 'external', approval_required: true, invocable: false },
+  { tool_key: 'playwright', source: 'mcp', description: 'Playwright MCP â€” headless browser control.', risk_class: 'external', approval_required: true, invocable: false },
+  { tool_key: 'context7', source: 'mcp', description: 'Context7 MCP â€” up-to-date library documentation (read).', risk_class: 'read', approval_required: false, invocable: false },
+  { tool_key: 'ref', source: 'mcp', description: 'Ref MCP â€” documentation search / URL reading (read).', risk_class: 'read', approval_required: false, invocable: false },
+  { tool_key: 'exa', source: 'mcp', description: 'Exa MCP â€” web/code/company research search (read).', risk_class: 'read', approval_required: false, invocable: false },
+
+  // --- Hermes ------------------------------------------------------------
+  { tool_key: 'hermes:tools', source: 'hermes', description: 'Hermes tool registry (70+ tools) via the Hermes API / mcp-serve.', risk_class: 'external', approval_required: true, invocable: false },
+  { tool_key: 'hermes:toolsets', source: 'hermes', description: 'Hermes toolsets (28 toolsets) â€” grouped capabilities.', risk_class: 'external', approval_required: true, invocable: false },
+
+  // --- Code-execution surfaces -------------------------------------------
+  { tool_key: 'codex', source: 'codex', description: 'Codex â€” senior-engineering code execution surface.', risk_class: 'write-local', approval_required: true, invocable: false },
+  { tool_key: 'claude-code', source: 'claude-code', description: 'Claude Code â€” agentic code execution surface.', risk_class: 'write-local', approval_required: true, invocable: false },
+] as const
+
+/** Candidate Hermes config locations (read-only). First existing wins. */
+function hermesConfigCandidates(): string[] {
+  const localAppData = process.env.LOCALAPPDATA ?? path.join(os.homedir(), 'AppData', 'Local')
+  return [
+    process.env.HERMES_CONFIG?.trim() || '',
+    path.join(localAppData, 'hermes', 'config.yaml'),
+    path.join(os.homedir(), '.hermes', 'config.yaml'),
+  ].filter(Boolean)
+}
+
+/**
+ * Read-only enrichment: extract MCP *server names only* from a Hermes config.
+ * Never reads values â€” only the keys directly under an `mcpServers:` / `mcp:`
+ * mapping â€” so no secret can leak. Returns [] on any error (best-effort).
+ */
+async function discoverHermesMcpServerNames(): Promise<string[]> {
+  for (const candidate of hermesConfigCandidates()) {
+    try {
+      const raw = await readFile(candidate, 'utf-8')
+      const names = parseMcpServerNames(raw)
+      if (names.length) return names
+    } catch {
+      // ignore â€” try the next candidate / fall back to the static set
+    }
+  }
+  return []
+}
+
+/**
+ * Minimal, dependency-free YAML scan for keys under an `mcpServers:` or `mcp:`
+ * block. Captures only the child key names (server identifiers), not values.
+ */
+export function parseMcpServerNames(yaml: string): string[] {
+  const lines = yaml.split(/\r?\n/)
+  const names: string[] = []
+  let inBlock = false
+  let blockIndent = -1
+
+  for (const line of lines) {
+    if (/^\s*#/.test(line) || line.trim() === '') continue
+    const indent = line.length - line.trimStart().length
+
+    if (!inBlock) {
+      if (/^\s*(mcpServers|mcp_servers|mcp)\s*:/.test(line)) {
+        inBlock = true
+        blockIndent = indent
+      }
+      continue
+    }
+
+    // A line at or below the block's own indent ends the block.
+    if (indent <= blockIndent) {
+      inBlock = false
+      blockIndent = -1
+      continue
+    }
+
+    // Direct children of the block (first nesting level) are server names.
+    const childMatch = line.match(/^\s+([A-Za-z0-9_.-]+)\s*:/)
+    if (childMatch && indent === blockIndent + 2) {
+      names.push(childMatch[1])
+    }
+  }
+
+  return Array.from(new Set(names))
+}
+
+/**
+ * Return the full read-only tool catalogue: the known static set plus any extra
+ * MCP server names discovered from the Hermes config. Nothing is ever invoked,
+ * and every entry has `invocable: false`.
+ */
+export async function getToolCatalogue(): Promise<CommandCentreTool[]> {
+  const known = [...KNOWN_TOOLS]
+  const knownKeys = new Set(known.map((t) => t.tool_key.toLowerCase()))
+
+  const discovered = await discoverHermesMcpServerNames()
+  for (const name of discovered) {
+    const key = name.toLowerCase()
+    if (knownKeys.has(key)) continue
+    knownKeys.add(key)
+    known.push({
+      tool_key: name,
+      source: 'mcp',
+      description: `MCP server discovered from Hermes config (read-only listing).`,
+      risk_class: 'external',
+      approval_required: true,
+      invocable: false,
+    })
+  }
+
+  return known
+}
+
+/** Synchronous accessor for the static known set (no filesystem access). */
+export function getKnownTools(): CommandCentreTool[] {
+  return [...KNOWN_TOOLS]
+}

+// src/lib/command-centre/wiki-graph.ts
+//
+// Pure graph-construction for the Wiki Graph View (UNI-2304).
+// Parses [[wikilink]] references out of wiki_pages content and resolves them,
+// case-insensitively, against the set of known pages (by slug/id OR title).
+// Unresolved links are dropped â€” never fabricated. Self-links are dropped.
+// No I/O here: the route supplies the rows, this module builds the graph so it
+// can be unit-tested offline.
+
+/** A wiki page row as needed for graph construction (subset of wiki_pages). */
+export interface WikiPageRow {
+  id: string
+  title: string
+  tags: string[] | null
+  content: string
+  updated_at: string | null
+}
+
+export interface GraphNode {
+  id: string
+  title: string
+  slug: string
+  tags: string[]
+  degree: number
+}
+
+export interface GraphEdge {
+  source: string
+  target: string
+}
+
+export interface WikiGraph {
+  nodes: GraphNode[]
+  edges: GraphEdge[]
+  pageCount: number
+  lastSync: string | null
+}
+
+const WIKILINK_RE = /\[\[([^\]]+)\]\]/g
+
+/**
+ * Extract the resolvable target of every [[wikilink]] in `content`.
+ * Handles Obsidian syntax: strips a `|alias` display label and a `#section`
+ * or `#^block` anchor, then trims. Empty targets are skipped.
+ */
+export function parseWikiLinks(content: string): string[] {
+  if (!content) return []
+  const out: string[] = []
+  for (const match of content.matchAll(WIKILINK_RE)) {
+    const raw = match[1]
+    // `[[target|alias]]` â†’ target ; `[[target#section]]` â†’ target
+    const target = raw.split('|')[0].split('#')[0].trim()
+    if (target.length > 0) out.push(target)
+  }
+  return out
+}
+
+/** Candidate lookup keys for a link target, mostâ†’least specific, lowercased. */
+function targetKeys(target: string): string[] {
+  const lower = target.toLowerCase()
+  const keys = [lower]
+  // Path-style links like `Sources/Some Page` also match on their basename.
+  const slash = lower.lastIndexOf('/')
+  if (slash !== -1 && slash < lower.length - 1) keys.push(lower.slice(slash + 1))
+  return keys
+}
+
+/**
+ * Build the wiki knowledge graph from page rows.
+ *
+ * - Nodes: one per page (orphans included), with undirected `degree`.
+ * - Edges: one per resolved, non-self [[wikilink]], de-duplicated per
+ *   directed (source, target) pair. Links that resolve to no known page are
+ *   dropped.
+ * - Resolution is case-insensitive against each page's id (slug) and title.
+ */
+export function buildWikiGraph(pages: WikiPageRow[]): WikiGraph {
+  // Resolve map: lowercased slug/title â†’ canonical node id.
+  // Slug (id) wins over title on collision â€” the id is the routing key.
+  const resolve = new Map<string, string>()
+  for (const p of pages) {
+    const titleKey = p.title?.toLowerCase().trim()
+    if (titleKey && !resolve.has(titleKey)) resolve.set(titleKey, p.id)
+  }
+  for (const p of pages) {
+    resolve.set(p.id.toLowerCase().trim(), p.id) // slug/id is authoritative
+  }
+
+  const degree = new Map<string, number>()
+  for (const p of pages) degree.set(p.id, 0)
+
+  const seen = new Set<string>()
+  const edges: GraphEdge[] = []
+  for (const p of pages) {
+    for (const target of parseWikiLinks(p.content)) {
+      let resolvedId: string | undefined
+      for (const key of targetKeys(target)) {
+        resolvedId = resolve.get(key)
+        if (resolvedId) break
+      }
+      if (!resolvedId || resolvedId === p.id) continue // unresolved or self-link
+      const dedupeKey = `${p.id}\u0000${resolvedId}`
+      if (seen.has(dedupeKey)) continue
+      seen.add(dedupeKey)
+      edges.push({ source: p.id, target: resolvedId })
+      degree.set(p.id, (degree.get(p.id) ?? 0) + 1)
+      degree.set(resolvedId, (degree.get(resolvedId) ?? 0) + 1)
+    }
+  }
+
+  const nodes: GraphNode[] = pages.map((p) => ({
+    id: p.id,
+    title: p.title,
+    slug: p.id,
+    tags: p.tags ?? [],
+    degree: degree.get(p.id) ?? 0,
+  }))
+
+  const lastSync = pages.reduce<string | null>((acc, p) => {
+    if (!p.updated_at) return acc
+    return acc === null || p.updated_at > acc ? p.updated_at : acc
+  }, null)
+
+  return { nodes, edges, pageCount: pages.length, lastSync }
+}

   1: /**
   2:  * proxy.ts â€” Next.js 16 network-boundary proxy.
   3:  * Node.js runtime required (crypto.subtle, Buffer).

   5:  * 1. Auth: verifies pi_session HMAC cookie â€” no Railway dependency.

  79: async function verifySession(token: string): Promise<boolean> {
  80:   if (!DASHBOARD_PASSWORD || !token) return false;
  81:   const dot = token.indexOf(".");
  82:   if (dot === -1) return false;
  83: 
  84:   const issuedAtStr = token.slice(0, dot);
  85:   const sig = token.slice(dot + 1);
  86:   const issuedAt = parseInt(issuedAtStr, 10);
  87:   if (isNaN(issuedAt)) return false;
  88: 
  89:   const age = Math.floor(Date.now() / 1000) - issuedAt;
  90:   if (age < 0 || age > SESSION_TTL_SECONDS) return false;
  91: 
  92:   const enc = new TextEncoder();
  93:   const cryptoKey = await crypto.subtle.importKey(
  94:     "raw",
  95:     enc.encode(DASHBOARD_PASSWORD),
  96:     { name: "HMAC", hash: "SHA-256" },
  97:     false,
  98:     ["sign"],
  99:   );
 100:   const expected = await crypto.subtle.sign("HMAC", cryptoKey, enc.encode(issuedAtStr));
 101:   const expectedHex = Array.from(new Uint8Array(expected))
 102:     .map((b) => b.toString(16).padStart(2, "0"))
 103:     .join("");
 104: 
 105:   return expectedHex === sig;
 106: }
 107: 
 108: function buildCsp(nonce: string): string {
 109:   const isDev = process.env.NODE_ENV === "development";
 110:   return [
 111:     "default-src 'self'",
 112:     `script-src 'self' 'nonce-${nonce}' 'wasm-unsafe-eval'${isDev ? " 'unsafe-eval'" : ""}`,
 113:     "style-src 'self' 'unsafe-inline' https://fonts.googleapis.com",
 114:     "font-src 'self' https://fonts.gstatic.com",
 115:     "connect-src 'self' https://api.github.com https://api.anthropic.com https://api.linear.app https://*.vercel.app https://*.supabase.co wss://*.supabase.co",
 116:     "worker-src blob:",
 117:     "img-src 'self' data: blob:",
 118:     "frame-ancestors 'none'",
 119:   ].join("; ");
 120: }
 121: 
 122: export async function proxy(req: NextRequest): Promise<NextResponse | Response> {
 123:   const { pathname } = req.nextUrl;
 124: 
 125:   // â”€â”€ Auth â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€
 126:   const isProtectedPage = PROTECTED_PAGE_PREFIXES.some((p) => pathname.startsWith(p));
 127:   const isPublicApi = PUBLIC_API_PREFIXES.some((p) => pathname.startsWith(p));
 128:   const isProtectedApi = !isPublicApi && PROTECTED_API_PREFIXES.some((p) => pathname.startsWith(p));
 129: 
 130:   if (isProtectedPage || isProtectedApi) {
 131:     const token = req.cookies.get(COOKIE_NAME)?.value ?? "";
 132:     const valid = await verifySession(token);
 133: 
 134:     if (!valid) {
 135:       if (isProtectedApi) {
 136:         return Response.json({ error: "Unauthorised" }, { status: 401 });
 137:       }
 138:       const loginUrl = new URL(LOGIN_PATH, req.url);
 139:       loginUrl.searchParams.set("redirect", pathname);
 140:       return NextResponse.redirect(loginUrl);
 141:     }
 142:   }
 143: 
 144:   // â”€â”€ CSP nonce â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€
 145:   const nonce = btoa(crypto.randomUUID()); // btoa works in Edge runtime; Buffer does not
 146:   const csp = buildCsp(nonce);
 147: 
 148:   const requestHeaders = new Headers(req.headers);
 149:   requestHeaders.set("x-nonce", nonce);
 150: 
 151:   const response = NextResponse.next({ request: { headers: requestHeaders } });

  12: // RLS-enforced, cookie-scoped client with per-user identity. The same specifier
  13: // in this app resolves to a service-role client that bypasses RLS, and this app
  14: // has no per-user identity at all â€” it is single-operator behind one shared

  16: // silently swapping an RLS-enforced read for an RLS-bypassing one.
  17: //

  19: // valid session. There is no getUser() equivalent here to call, and inventing one
  20: // would be a fabricated identity rather than a port.
  21: //
  22: // That sentence was WRONG when first written â€” it said the proxy's matcher "covers

  30: import { NextResponse } from "next/server";
  31: import { createServerClient } from "@/lib/supabase/server";
  32: import { buildWikiGraph, type WikiPageRow } from "@/lib/command-centre/wiki-graph";
  33: 
  34: export const dynamic = "force-dynamic";
  35: 
  36: // PostgREST's default row cap. Made explicit so truncation can be DETECTED and
  37: // surfaced honestly, instead of silently rendering a partial graph as complete.
  38: const WIKI_PAGES_LIMIT = 1000;
  39: 
  40: export async function GET(): Promise<Response> {
  41:   try {
  42:     const supabase = createServerClient();
  43: 
  44:     const { data, error } = await supabase
  45:       .from("wiki_pages")
  46:       .select("id,title,tags,content,updated_at")
  47:       .limit(WIKI_PAGES_LIMIT);
  48: 
  49:     if (error) {
  50:       // Message only â€” never the full error object, which can carry connection
  51:       // details and query text.
  52:       return NextResponse.json(
  53:         { error: "Failed to read wiki pages", detail: error.message },
  54:         { status: 500 }
  55:       );
  56:     }
  57: 
  58:     const pages = (data ?? []) as WikiPageRow[];
  59:     const graph = buildWikiGraph(pages);
  60: 
  61:     // If we hit the cap, the graph is a partial view. Say so rather than letting
  62:     // a truncated graph read as the whole knowledge base.
  63:     const truncated = pages.length >= WIKI_PAGES_LIMIT;
  64: 
  65:     return NextResponse.json({
  66:       ...graph,
  67:       pageCount: pages.length,
  68:       truncated,
  69:       ...(truncated
  70:         ? {
  71:             warning:
  72:               `Only the first ${WIKI_PAGES_LIMIT} pages were read, so this graph is ` +
  73:               `incomplete. Nodes and edges beyond the cap are missing, not absent.`,
  74:           }
  75:         : {}),
  76:     });
  77:   } catch (e) {
  78:     // Do NOT pass e.message through. The adjacent handler above is deliberately
  79:     // message-only for a *known* PostgREST error shape; this catch-all sees
  80:     // anything at all â€” client construction, env resolution, a thrown driver
  81:     // error â€” and those messages carry connection strings and query text. Log
  82:     // server-side, return a fixed string.
  83:     console.error("[wiki-graph] unhandled failure building graph:", e);
  84:     return NextResponse.json({ error: "Failed to build wiki graph" }, { status: 500 });
  85:   }
  86: }

  29: /** A page/API request carrying NO session cookie. */
  30: const anonymous = (path: string) => new NextRequest(new URL(path, BASE));
  31: 
  32: /** What did the proxy do â€” send them to login, refuse, or let them through? */
  33: async function outcome(path: string): Promise<"redirect" | "401" | "through"> {
  34:   const res = await proxy(anonymous(path));
  35:   if (res.status === 401) return "401";
  36:   // NextResponse.redirect() is a 307/308 with a location header.
  37:   if (res.status >= 300 && res.status < 400 && res.headers.get("location")) return "redirect";
  38:   return "through";
  39: }

 succeeded in 415ms:
   1: /**
   2:  * command-centre-readonly.test.ts â€” diff-relative conformance for ported capabilities.
   3:  *
   4:  * THE CLAIM THIS TEST CHECKS, and the one it deliberately does NOT:
   5:  *
   6:  *   CHECKS:      "this port introduces no network/DB/execution construct that the
   7:  *                 named source baseline did not already contain."
   8:  *   DOES NOT:    "this page makes no network call."
   9:  *
  10:  * The second is an unbounded negative. Three bounded attempts at capability 1 were
  11:  * all correctly failed by cross-vendor review because the spec asked for it: there
  12:  * is always another path (dynamic import, require, WebSocket, sendBeacon, server
  13:  * action, transitive side effect). The code was fine every time; the spec was the
  14:  * defect. Founder ruling 2026-08-01: take the diff-relative framing.
  15:  *
  16:  * A diff-relative claim is decidable â€” compare against a baseline, answer yes or no.
  17:  * It is also only as strong as that baseline, which is why capabilities whose safety
  18:  * properties are load-bearing (operator-gateway) require a hand-established baseline
  19:  * recorded in the provenance file before they may be ported.
  20:  */
  21: import { describe, expect, it } from "vitest";
  22: import { existsSync, readFileSync, readdirSync, statSync } from "node:fs";
  23: import { dirname, join, resolve } from "node:path";
  24: 
  25: const ROOT = resolve(__dirname, "..");
  26: const PROV_RAW = JSON.parse(
  27:   readFileSync(join(__dirname, "command-centre-provenance.json"), "utf8")
  28: ) as Record<string, unknown>;
  29: const PROV = PROV_RAW as unknown as {
  30:   _baseline_root: string;
  31:   files: Record<string, string>;
  32:   /** Written fresh for this app. No source baseline exists, so diff-relative
  33:    *  comparison is meaningless for these â€” they are declared, not compared. */
  34:   _rebuilt_not_ported?: Record<string, string>;
  35:   /** Pre-existing files of THIS app, pulled into the graph by an import. */
  36:   _target_native?: Record<string, string>;
  37:   /** file -> rule -> the ONE expected count change, with its reason. */
  38:   _declared_deltas?: Record<string, Record<string, DeclaredDelta>>;
  39: };
  40: 
  41: /** An exemption names the exact magnitude it excuses, not just the direction. */
  42: interface DeclaredDelta {
  43:   /** Count in the baseline. */
  44:   from: number;
  45:   /** Count the port is allowed to have â€” this value and no other. */
  46:   to: number;
  47:   reason: string;
  48: }
  49: 
  50: /**
  51:  * True only for the EXACT declared count change.
  52:  *
  53:  * This used to key on file+rule alone, which made it a blanket exclusion wearing
  54:  * a narrower label: declaring "auth gate 3 -> 0" also excused 3 -> 1, 3 -> 2, and
  55:  * every future auth-gate loss in that file. Cross-vendor review caught it â€”
  56:  * "exempts any auth gate decrease in wiki-graph/page.tsx, not just the expected
  57:  * removal". The mechanism existed to avoid a blanket exclusion and was blanket one
  58:  * level down. An exemption now excuses one measured transition; anything else in
  59:  * the same file, same rule, still fails.
  60:  */
  61: const deltaDeclared = (file: string, rule: string, from: number, to: number): boolean => {
  62:   const d = (PROV_RAW as {
  63:     _declared_deltas?: Record<string, Record<string, DeclaredDelta>>;
  64:   })._declared_deltas?.[file]?.[rule];
  65:   if (!d) return false;
  66:   return d.from === from && d.to === to && Boolean(d.reason);
  67: };
  68: 
  69: /** Files with a declared origin OR a declared reason for having none. */
  70: const DECLARED = new Set<string>([
  71:   ...Object.keys((PROV_RAW as { files: Record<string, string> }).files),
  72:   ...Object.keys((PROV_RAW as { _rebuilt_not_ported?: Record<string, string> })._rebuilt_not_ported ?? {}),
  73:   ...Object.keys((PROV_RAW as { _target_native?: Record<string, string> })._target_native ?? {}),
  74: ]);
  75: const BASELINE = PROV._baseline_root;
  76: const baselineAvailable = existsSync(BASELINE);
  77: 
  78: /** Constructs whose COUNT must not increase relative to the baseline. */
  79: const TRACKED: Array<{ re: RegExp; rule: string }> = [
  80:   { re: /\bfetch\s*\(/g, rule: "network: fetch" },
  81:   { re: /\b(axios|got|node-fetch|undici)\b/g, rule: "network: http client" },
  82:   { re: /\b(WebSocket|EventSource|XMLHttpRequest|sendBeacon)\b/g, rule: "network: streaming/beacon" },
  83:   { re: /\bimport\s*\(/g, rule: "dynamic import" },
  84:   { re: /\brequire\s*\(/g, rule: "require()" },
  85:   { re: /https?:\/\//g, rule: "remote host literal" },
  86:   { re: /\.(insert|update|upsert|delete)\s*\(/g, rule: "database write" },
  87:   { re: /createServerClient|createClient\s*\(/g, rule: "database client" },
  88:   { re: /\bMcpClient\b|modelcontextprotocol/g, rule: "MCP client" },
  89:   { re: /"use server"|'use server'/g, rule: "server action" },
  90:   { re: /ANTHROPIC_API_KEY|OPENAI_API_KEY|STRIPE_SECRET/g, rule: "paid API key" },
  91:   // OG2 had zero coverage until this line. External execution was tracked by nothing.
  92:   { re: /\b(child_process|execSync|spawnSync|\bexec\s*\(|\bspawn\s*\(|\bfork\s*\()/g,
  93:     rule: "external execution" },
  94: ];
  95: 
  96: /**
  97:  * Guards whose count must NOT DECREASE. The tracked list above is one-directional â€”
  98:  * it fails on an increase â€” so a port that DELETES a safety check passes every one of
  99:  * them. Cross-vendor audit named this as the single most valuable missing check:
 100:  * "a port that does not add new scary constructs, but removes the exact guards that
 101:  * made the scary surface inert."
 102:  */
 103: const GUARDS: Array<{ re: RegExp; rule: string }> = [
 104:   { re: /\b(dryRun|dry_run|DRY_RUN|isDryRun)\b/g, rule: "dry-run predicate" },
 105:   { re: /\bdisabled\b/g, rule: "disabled control" },
 106:   { re: /\b(readOnly|read_only|READONLY)\b/g, rule: "read-only flag" },
 107:   { re: /\b(requireAuth|isAuthed|getUser|getSession|redirect\s*\(\s*['"]\/auth)/g, rule: "auth gate" },
 108:   { re: /\b(sandbox|SANDBOX|isSandbox)\b/g, rule: "sandbox boundary" },
 109:   { re: /\bconfirm\s*\(|\bconfirmed\b/g, rule: "confirmation gate" },
 110: ];
 111: 
 112: const strip = (s: string) =>
 113:   s.replace(/\/\*[\s\S]*?\*\//g, "").replace(/^\s*\/\/.*$/gm, "");
 114: 
 115: const count = (src: string, re: RegExp) => (strip(src).match(new RegExp(re.source, "g")) || []).length;
 116: 
 117: function resolveSpec(spec: string, from: string): string | null {
 118:   let base: string;
 119:   if (spec.startsWith("@/")) base = join(ROOT, spec.slice(2));
 120:   else if (spec.startsWith(".")) base = resolve(dirname(from), spec);
 121:   else return null;
 122:   for (const c of [`${base}.ts`, `${base}.tsx`, join(base, "index.ts"), join(base, "index.tsx"), base]) {
 123:     if (existsSync(c) && statSync(c).isFile()) return c;
 124:   }
 125:   return null;
 126: }
 127: 
 128: /** Everything reachable from the capability entry pages, following imports. */
 129: function importGraph(): string[] {
 130:   const entries: string[] = [];
 131:   (function walk(d: string) {
 132:     if (!existsSync(d)) return;
 133:     for (const e of readdirSync(d)) {
 134:       const p = join(d, e);
 135:       if (statSync(p).isDirectory()) walk(p);
 136:       else if (e === "page.tsx") entries.push(p);
 137:     }
 138:   })(join(ROOT, "app/(main)/command-centre"));
 139: 
 140:   const seen = new Set<string>();
 141:   const queue = [...entries];
 142:   while (queue.length) {
 143:     const f = queue.pop()!;
 144:     if (seen.has(f) || !existsSync(f)) continue;
 145:     seen.add(f);
 146:     // Both `import x from 'y'` and the side-effect form `import 'y'`. Matching only
 147:     // `from '...'` let a side-effect import enter the surface unprovenanced.
 148:     for (const m of readFileSync(f, "utf8").matchAll(/(?:from|import)\s+['"]([^'"]+)['"]/g)) {
 149:       const r = resolveSpec(m[1], f);
 150:       if (r && !seen.has(r)) queue.push(r);
 151:     }
 152:   }
 153:   return [...seen];
 154: }
 155: 
 156: const rel = (f: string) => f.slice(ROOT.length + 1).replace(/\\/g, "/");
 157: 
 158: describe("command-centre: no new surface vs source baseline", () => {
 159:   const files = importGraph().map(rel);
 160: 
 161:   it("the import graph reaches beyond the capability directory (positive control)", () => {
 162:     expect(files.length).toBeGreaterThan(1);
 163:     expect(files.some((f) => f.startsWith("components/command-centre"))).toBe(true);
 164:   });
 165: 
 166:   it("every file in the capability surface has a declared origin", () => {
 167:     // A file with no provenance has no baseline, so "no new surface" is unprovable
 168:     // for it. Unlisted means fail, not skip.
 169:     const undeclared = files.filter((f) => !DECLARED.has(f));
 170:     expect(undeclared, `no provenance entry for:\n${undeclared.join("\n")}`).toEqual([]);
 171:   });
 172: 
 173:   it("the baseline is reachable, or the run is explicitly acknowledged as unverified", () => {
 174:     // A skipped comparison is not a passing one. Previously this only warned while
 175:     // the suite still went green â€” so an environment without the baseline reported
 176:     // success having checked nothing. It now FAILS closed. Setting
 177:     // CC_ALLOW_NO_BASELINE=1 is the only way past, and it makes the gap a recorded
 178:     // choice rather than a silent one.
 179:     const acknowledged = process.env.CC_ALLOW_NO_BASELINE === "1";
 180:     expect(
 181:       baselineAvailable || acknowledged,
 182:       `[BASELINE UNAVAILABLE] ${BASELINE} not present, so the diff-relative claim ` +
 183:         `was NOT verified. This suite refuses to report success without checking. ` +
 184:         `Provide the baseline checkout, or set CC_ALLOW_NO_BASELINE=1 to record ` +
 185:         `deliberately that this run proves nothing about R2/R6.`
 186:     ).toBe(true);
 187:   });
 188: 
 189:   it("detects an increase when one exists (positive control)", () => {
 190:     const before = count("const a = 1", /\bfetch\s*\(/);
 191:     const after = count("const a = fetch('x')", /\bfetch\s*\(/);
 192:     expect(after).toBeGreaterThan(before);
 193:   });
 194: 
 195:   // ---- G1: every internal link/fetch must resolve to a route that EXISTS here ----
 196:   // Construct counts are identical when a ported fetch points at an API route that was
 197:   // never ported, so the surface passes while the control 404s. Found in capability 2
 198:   // by the reviewer, not by the harness.
 199:   it("every internal href/fetch resolves to a route in the target app", () => {
 200:     const APP = join(ROOT, "app");
 201:     const routeExists = (p: string): boolean => {
 202:       const clean = p.split("?")[0].split("#")[0].replace(/\/$/, "");
 203:       if (!clean || clean === "/") return true;
 204:       const segs = clean.split("/").filter(Boolean);
 205:       // Next.js route groups "(main)" are transparent in the URL, so try every
 206:       // group-prefixed location as well as the bare one.
 207:       const groups = existsSync(APP)
 208:         ? readdirSync(APP).filter((d) => d.startsWith("(") && statSync(join(APP, d)).isDirectory())
 209:         : [];
 210:       const bases = [APP, ...groups.map((g) => join(APP, g))];
 211:       return bases.some((b) => {
 212:         const dir = join(b, ...segs);
 213:         return existsSync(join(dir, "page.tsx")) || existsSync(join(dir, "route.ts"));
 214:       });
 215:     };
 216: 
 217:     const broken: string[] = [];
 218:     for (const f of files) {
 219:       const src = strip(readFileSync(join(ROOT, f), "utf8"));
 220:       const paths = [
 221:         ...[...src.matchAll(/href=["'`](\/[^"'`]*)["'`]/g)].map((m) => m[1]),
 222:         ...[...src.matchAll(/fetch\(\s*["'`](\/[^"'`]*)["'`]/g)].map((m) => m[1]),
 223:       ];
 224:       for (const p of new Set(paths)) {
 225:         if (!routeExists(p)) broken.push(`${f} -> ${p}`);
 226:       }
 227:     }
 228:     expect(
 229:       broken,
 230:       `internal paths with no matching route in the target app (these would 404):\n${broken.join("\n")}`
 231:     ).toEqual([]);
 232:   });
 233: 
 234:   // ---- G10 / Q3: a port may not DELETE a guard ----
 235:   // fail-open-ok: same reasoning as the tracked-construct tests â€” the separate
 236:   // "baseline is reachable" test FAILS when the baseline is absent, so the suite
 237:   // cannot go green unverified. This skip avoids a second misleading failure; it
 238:   // does not hide the first.
 239:   it.skipIf(!baselineAvailable)("no safety guard was removed relative to the baseline", () => {
 240:     const lost: string[] = [];
 241:     for (const g of GUARDS) {
 242:       for (const [ported, source] of Object.entries(PROV.files)) {
 243:         const pPath = join(ROOT, ported);
 244:         const sPath = join(BASELINE, source);
 245:         if (!existsSync(pPath) || !existsSync(sPath)) continue; // covered by the unresolved check
 246:         const p = count(readFileSync(pPath, "utf8"), g.re);
 247:         const s = count(readFileSync(sPath, "utf8"), g.re);
 248:         if (p < s && !deltaDeclared(ported, g.rule, s, p)) lost.push(`${g.rule} in ${ported}: ${s} -> ${p}`);
 249:       }
 250:     }
 251:     expect(lost, `safety guards removed vs baseline:\n${lost.join("\n")}`).toEqual([]);
 252:   });
 253: 
 254:   for (const t of TRACKED) {
 255:     // fail-open-ok: skipping here is safe because the separate "baseline is
 256:     // reachable" test FAILS when the baseline is absent, so the suite cannot go
 257:     // green unverified. The skip avoids a misleading second failure, it does not
 258:     // hide the first.
 259:     it.skipIf(!baselineAvailable)(`introduces no new â€” ${t.rule}`, () => {
 260:       const grew: string[] = [];
 261:       const unresolved: string[] = [];
 262:       for (const [ported, source] of Object.entries(PROV.files)) {
 263:         const pPath = join(ROOT, ported);
 264:         const sPath = join(BASELINE, source);
 265:         // A pair that cannot be resolved must NOT be skipped. Skipping it left the
 266:         // file uncompared while the suite still went green â€” a mistyped baseline
 267:         // path would read as "verified clean" having checked nothing.
 268:         if (!existsSync(pPath)) { unresolved.push(`ported missing: ${ported}`); continue; }
 269:         if (!existsSync(sPath)) { unresolved.push(`baseline missing: ${source} (for ${ported})`); continue; }
 270:         const p = count(readFileSync(pPath, "utf8"), t.re);
 271:         const s = count(readFileSync(sPath, "utf8"), t.re);
 272:         if (p > s && !deltaDeclared(ported, t.rule, s, p)) grew.push(`${ported}: ${s} -> ${p}`);
 273:       }
 274:       expect(
 275:         unresolved,
 276:         `provenance pairs could not be resolved, so these files were NOT compared:\n${unresolved.join("\n")}`
 277:       ).toEqual([]);
 278:       expect(grew, `${t.rule} increased vs baseline:\n${grew.join("\n")}`).toEqual([]);
 279:     });
 280:   }
 281: });
 282: 
 283: /**
 284:  * Import-level provenance. File provenance proves a file has a declared origin; it
 285:  * does NOT prove that what its imports RESOLVE TO behaves the same in both apps.
 286:  * `@/lib/supabase/server` resolves in the source to an anon-key RLS-enforced client
 287:  * and in the target to a service-role client that bypasses RLS â€” same specifier,
 288:  * compatible shapes, clean typecheck, silent privilege change.
 289:  */
 290: describe("command-centre: every import has a declared judgment", () => {
 291:   const IMPORTS = (PROV as unknown as {
 292:     imports?: Record<string, { specifier: string; judgment: string; note: string }>;
 293:   }).imports ?? {};
 294: 
 295:   it("the import map is populated (positive control)", () => {
 296:     // An empty map would make every assertion below vacuously true.
 297:     expect(Object.keys(IMPORTS).length).toBeGreaterThan(0);
 298:   });
 299: 
 300:   it("no import is UNDECLARED", () => {
 301:     const undeclared = Object.entries(IMPORTS)
 302:       .filter(([, v]) => v.judgment === "UNDECLARED")
 303:       .map(([k, v]) => `${k}\n      ${v.note}`);
 304:     expect(
 305:       undeclared,
 306:       `imports with no declared judgment (same | different-but-checked | must-change):\n${undeclared.join("\n")}`
 307:     ).toEqual([]);
 308:   });
 309: 
 310:   it("every judgment is one of the permitted values", () => {
 311:     const ok = new Set(["same", "different-but-checked", "must-change", "no-source-baseline"]);
 312:     const bad = Object.entries(IMPORTS)
 313:       .filter(([, v]) => !ok.has(v.judgment))
 314:       .map(([k, v]) => `${k}: '${v.judgment}'`);
 315:     expect(bad, `invalid judgment values:\n${bad.join("\n")}`).toEqual([]);
 316:   });
 317: 
 318:   /**
 319:    * `no-source-baseline` is the honest judgment for an import in a file that has no
 320:    * source counterpart â€” a rebuilt or target-native file. Comparing its imports across
 321:    * apps is not a check that can be run, and labelling it `must-change` would assert a
 322:    * comparison that never happened.
 323:    *
 324:    * It is also, obviously, an escape hatch: any import could be waved through by
 325:    * claiming its file has no baseline. So it is CONSTRAINED to files actually declared
 326:    * as baseline-free. This is the third time on this capability that a mechanism built
 327:    * to narrow an exclusion had to be narrowed itself â€” the pattern is that an exemption
 328:    * is only as tight as the thing that decides who may claim it.
 329:    */
 330:   it("'no-source-baseline' is only claimable by a file declared as having none", () => {
 331:     const baselineFree = new Set([
 332:       ...Object.keys((PROV_RAW as { _rebuilt_not_ported?: Record<string, string> })._rebuilt_not_ported ?? {}),
 333:       ...Object.keys((PROV_RAW as { _target_native?: Record<string, string> })._target_native ?? {}),
 334:     ]);
 335:     const bad = Object.entries(IMPORTS)
 336:       .filter(([, v]) => v.judgment === "no-source-baseline")
 337:       .map(([k]) => k)
 338:       .filter((k) => !baselineFree.has(k.split(" :: ")[0]));
 339:     expect(
 340:       bad,
 341:       "these claim 'no-source-baseline' but their file IS declared with a source baseline,\n" +
 342:         "so a real import comparison was owed and skipped:\n" + bad.join("\n")
 343:     ).toEqual([]);
 344:   });
 345: 
 346:   it("a 'different-but-checked' judgment carries a stated reason", () => {
 347:     const unexplained = Object.entries(IMPORTS)
 348:       .filter(([, v]) => v.judgment !== "same" && !v.note.trim())
 349:       .map(([k]) => k);
 350:     expect(unexplained, `divergent imports with no note:\n${unexplained.join("\n")}`).toEqual([]);
 351:   });
 352: 
 353:   /**
 354:    * ---- The map must match reality, in BOTH directions ----
 355:    *
 356:    * Everything above validates the map against itself. Cross-vendor review found what
 357:    * that permits: an entry for `knowledge/page.tsx :: @/components/command-centre/
 358:    * WikiEnhanceControl`, judged `must-change`, resolving to a target file that does
 359:    * not exist â€” for a component KI-002 deliberately omitted and the page never
 360:    * imports. Four assertions passed over it. A phantom entry does not merely sit
 361:    * there being wrong; it INFLATES the map, and the map is the thing we point at to
 362:    * say the import surface was reviewed. It reads as coverage.
 363:    *
 364:    * Deleting that one entry fixes one entry. These two tests fix the class: the map
 365:    * cannot claim an import that no file makes, and no file can make an import the map
 366:    * does not claim. Either direction failing is a real finding â€” the first means the
 367:    * map overstates what was checked, the second means something entered the surface
 368:    * unreviewed.
 369:    */
 370:   const ACTUAL = new Set<string>(
 371:     importGraph().flatMap((f) =>
 372:       [...readFileSync(f, "utf8").matchAll(/(?:from|import)\s+['"]([^'"]+)['"]/g)]
 373:         .map((m) => `${rel(f)} :: ${m[1]}`)
 374:     )
 375:   );
 376: 
 377:   it("the actual-import set is populated (positive control)", () => {
 378:     // Both directional tests below compare against ACTUAL. If the graph walk or the
 379:     // specifier regex breaks, ACTUAL empties, and "no phantom entries" would still
 380:     // pass while checking nothing. This is the check that stops that being silent.
 381:     expect(ACTUAL.size).toBeGreaterThan(0);
 382:   });
 383: 
 384:   it("no map entry describes an import that is not actually made", () => {
 385:     const phantom = Object.keys(IMPORTS).filter((k) => !ACTUAL.has(k));
 386:     expect(
 387:       phantom,
 388:       "provenance map entries with no corresponding import in the file named by the key.\n" +
 389:         "The map overstates what was reviewed â€” these were 'checked' and do not exist:\n" +
 390:         phantom.join("\n")
 391:     ).toEqual([]);
 392:   });
 393: 
 394:   it("every actual import in the capability graph has a map entry", () => {
 395:     const unmapped = [...ACTUAL].filter((k) => !(k in IMPORTS));
 396:     expect(
 397:       unmapped,
 398:       "imports present in the capability graph with no provenance entry â€” these entered\n" +
 399:         "the surface without a declared judgment:\n" + unmapped.join("\n")
 400:     ).toEqual([]);
 401:   });
 402: 
 403:   it("a map entry resolving to a target file names a file that exists", () => {
 404:     const missing = Object.entries(IMPORTS)
 405:       .map(([k, v]) => [k, /^file:\s*(.+)$/.exec(
 406:         (v as unknown as { resolves_in_target?: string }).resolves_in_target ?? ""
 407:       )] as const)
 408:       .filter(([, m]) => m && !existsSync(join(ROOT, m[1].trim())))
 409:       .map(([k, m]) => `${k} -> ${m![1].trim()}`);
 410:     expect(
 411:       missing,
 412:       `map entries resolving to a target file that is absent from disk:\n${missing.join("\n")}`
 413:     ).toEqual([]);
 414:   });
 415: });

**Axis 1 — Standards**

Hard issue: [WikiGraphCanvas.tsx](/d:/Pi-Dev-Ops/dashboard/components/command-centre/wiki-graph/WikiGraphCanvas.tsx:322) routes node clicks to `/founder/wiki/${slug}`. This app has no `dashboard/app/**/founder/wiki/**/page.tsx`; the only top-level app dirs are `(main)`, `api`, `favicon.ico`, and `sw.js`. That is a broken internal navigation control.

The declared `pointerleave` leak at [WikiGraphCanvas.tsx](/d:/Pi-Dev-Ops/dashboard/components/command-centre/wiki-graph/WikiGraphCanvas.tsx:330) and the single-line `react-hooks/refs` suppression at [WikiGraphCanvas.tsx](/d:/Pi-Dev-Ops/dashboard/components/command-centre/wiki-graph/WikiGraphCanvas.tsx:382) are defensible under the disclosed “faithful port, declare-not-fix” ruling. The suppression is scoped to the one offending `left:` expression, so a new ref-read elsewhere should still fail lint.

I could not run the Vitest suites independently: both named commands failed loading `vitest.config.ts` with Vite `[plugin externalize-deps] Error: spawn EPERM`, matching the brief’s caveat.

**Axis 2 — Spec**

FAIL on the named attempt-2 response. The two-direction import map check closes the specific `WikiEnhanceControl` phantom entry for page-imported files, but not the class. `importGraph()` starts only from `app/(main)/command-centre/**/page.tsx` at [command-centre-readonly.test.ts](/d:/Pi-Dev-Ops/dashboard/__tests__/command-centre-readonly.test.ts:129). The API route is only checked for existence via literal `href`/`fetch` scanning at [command-centre-readonly.test.ts](/d:/Pi-Dev-Ops/dashboard/__tests__/command-centre-readonly.test.ts:199), not added to `ACTUAL`.

Concrete stale-map case that passes: add or change an import in [route.ts](/d:/Pi-Dev-Ops/dashboard/app/api/command-centre/wiki-graph/route.ts:30). Its imports of `next/server`, `@/lib/supabase/server`, and `@/lib/command-centre/wiki-graph` have no `app/api/command-centre/wiki-graph/route.ts :: ...` entries; the provenance file only declares the route as `_rebuilt_not_ported` at [command-centre-provenance.json](/d:/Pi-Dev-Ops/dashboard/__tests__/command-centre-provenance.json:298). Therefore “no real import without an entry” is false for a capability surface file.

`no-source-baseline` is acceptable only if independently constrained; here the constraint is still provenance-file self-assertion. That is not fatal by itself, but combined with the route omission it becomes an escape hatch. The auth prefix fix appears to protect `/command-centre/**` and `/api/command-centre/**`; API returns 401, not redirect.
